D21 is NOT complete and Ledger Signature is NOT accepted.** Gate verified first: D20.1B is COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED as `21a20a144d3f81c15ab95090b485f6241abf1401` (`feat(ui): complete native confirmation migration`; Actions run #113 success, read from the public API; `main` = `origin/main`); only the three protected untracked files were present. **Workstream A (Signature as the default) was deliberately NOT built:** a Classic choice is stored as nothing (`savePalette` removes the keys), so an explicit Classic choice cannot be told from never choosing; flipping would silently restyle every Classic device and a Classic backup would restore as Signature on a fresh device. Palette and mode are device-only (not synced); backups carry the palette only when one was chosen. Four options are in the report (section 5); the recommended one is two steps (save Classic explicitly and pin devices that already have data, then flip in a later release). **Built:** the browser-bar colour is set before first paint from the saved mode and palette (`index.html`; Signature light `#EEF1F6`, dark `#0B1220`, Classic `#1f6feb` / `#12151a`; `THEME_COLOR` and the manifest unchanged); `.an-tablewrap` is now positioned so screen-reader-only text in a wide table cannot widen the page (Income was 1033 px wide at an 800 px window); Income Deductions table wrapped in the scroll box; 18 unnamed fields on Income and Debt payoff named. Report: `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D21-REPORT.md`; pictures: `Documents\Ledger-Design-Concepts\D21-Evidence`. **Next action: owner decisions in the report (default strategy, bar colour, alert and transaction follow-ups, touch targets, real-device plan), then a recommended D21.x.**

> **LEDGER SIGNATURE D18.1 (PERSON-CENTRIC INCOME ORGANIZATION), 2026-11-13 (superseded by the D19 notice above; COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED `18f58bd`, run #107 success): BUILT, TESTED AND VISUALLY REVIEWED; RELEASE AUTHORIZED AND COMMITTED AS `feat(ui): organize Income by person` (SHA `18f58bd`, Actions run #107 success and Pages deployment `7772335102` success were verified at the D19 gate); grant worksheet and Add to holdings smoke-checked; NOT REAL-DEVICE OR SCREEN-READER TESTED; D19 NOT STARTED.** Report: `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D18.1-REPORT.md`. Release gate: D18 is COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED as `e42361373a4f8fa10bc86417cef1732032e5d24c` (Actions run #106 success; Pages deployment `7770485329` success for the same SHA; read from the public GitHub API because `gh` is not installed; `main` = `origin/main`; the `PercentField` correction is in that commit), so every older "D18 uncommitted" statement is superseded. The Income page is now organized Household, then Person, then Compensation details: the navy household panel stays on top; People stays a compact section (Income people are kept separate from Family and are not matched to it); one independently expandable workspace per Income person holds that person's year summary, pay setup (the existing `ProfileEditor`), bonuses, stock awards (RSU grants, vests, withholding, Add to holdings) and paycheck detail; household tax settings and results stay at household level. The folded header shows the model's own figures (gross, take-home, bonus, stock awards "counted when they vest") or "Not set up for {year}". Fold state is remembered per device under `income.person.<personId>`; first person open by default. `RsuSection` and `RsuGrants` gained optional `personId` and `unassigned` props (records found by stored id only; a new vest or grant starts with the workspace's person; only editing an existing record can deliberately move it). Records whose person is missing are listed in a Needs review section and never repaired automatically. **No engine, schema, sync, backup, Search, Quick Add or navigation file changed; nothing in `src/lib` changed.** One shared change to review: `Collapsible` can now remember an "opened" choice for a section that starts folded. Checks: new `d181PersonIncome.test.tsx` (24 tests); 632 targeted tests in 33 files pass; `tsc` and `npm run build` clean; full suite NOT run locally (CI will); browser checks at 1440, 375 and 320 in Signature Light, Dark and Classic, with real clicks for fold state, jump, add bonus, add vest, add grant, deliberate reassignment, year change, a person with no setup and orphan records; every authoritative amount and input from the D18 page is present after (43 distinct amounts, 71 input values). Not driven: deletes, Add to holdings, worksheets, saving profiles, keyboard Tab walk, screen reader, real phone. Pictures: `Documents\Ledger-Design-Concepts\D18.1-Evidence`.

> **D18 RELEASE AUTHORIZED AND PLAN FOR D18.1 (2026-11-13, superseded by the D18.1 notice above):** the owner approved all ten D18 pages and the `PercentField` display correction (Retirement confidence now shows the saved value when settings load late; it never overwrites an active edit and never writes on load) and authorized the commit and deployment of D18 (SHA and CI result are recorded in the release report and the next change to this file). **D18.1, Person-Centric Income Organization (now BUILT LOCALLY, see the D18.1 notice above).** Sequence: D18 release, then D18.1, then D19, D20, D21. Goal: organize Income around the person each compensation item belongs to instead of household-wide sections by type. Layout: keep the household income summary at the top; one independently expandable workspace per person (several can be open at once) grouping that person's salary and paycheck setup, bonuses, RSU and stock awards, deductions, benefits and contributions; a concise compensation summary in each collapsed header; genuinely household-wide settings (filing assumptions, shared tax settings) stay outside the person containers; ownership of every item is obvious. Safeguards: reuse existing person associations; no automatic reassignment of records; no matching or merging people by name; no change to compensation, RSU, payroll or tax calculations; no schema or migration unless separately justified and approved; vested versus unvested equity keeps its meaning; every existing form, control and record is preserved.

## 2. Project goals and user experience

Ledger aims to be one cohesive household system, covering money, planning, activities, projects, possessions, upkeep, service relationships, bills, subscriptions, family coordination, contacts, documents, and eventually useful AI assistance. It is currently designed for one household, not a multi-tenant SaaS.

Design goals:

1. Simple everyday workflows, progressive disclosure, practical utility before complexity.
2. Local-first; data belongs to the household, with explicit controls over what may be shared externally.
3. One source of truth per domain; **link** records across modules instead of duplicating them.
4. Existing deterministic Finance calculations remain authoritative; AI, charts and summaries **must not invent or recalculate balances**.
5. Preserve historical records, deletion safety, backup/restore and synchronization behavior.
6. Desktop and phone (~375px), dark mode, keyboard/accessibility, clear errors, and cautious confirmation for destructive operations.
7. Keep development phased and reviewable; avoid broad refactors, unneeded dependencies, premature platforms/backends.

## 3. Roles and standard development loop

**Owner:** Sets priorities, approves material product decisions, authorizes commits/pushes, performs real-device acceptance. Explain consequences plainly and recommend defaults rather than asking technical questions without guidance.

**ChatGPT:** Product partner, architect, skeptic and reviewer. Drafts detailed implementation prompts for Claude; examines plan/reports against prior decisions and architectural risk; demands tests/evidence; issues explicit **APPROVED TO COMMIT/PUSH**, **APPROVED WITH FOLLOW-UPS**, or **HOLD — FIX BEFORE COMMIT** verdicts. Does not assert it has inspected uncommitted files without receiving them. Makes pragmatic decisions about when AI adds value. May verify committed GitHub repository and Actions when connected.

**Claude:** Works in the user's local repository; reads source and docs; implements only authorized scope; runs tests/typecheck/build and isolated browser checks with invented data; writes detailed dated implementation reports and updates backlog; **does not commit or push until specifically authorized**. Pushing `main` publishes the app; confirm green Actions afterwards.

**Normal workflow** (continue this pattern):

1. Plan/design milestone (when architectural questions are material); one concise recommended decision round if necessary.
2. Owner approves design and ChatGPT provides precise, bounded Claude prompt.
3. Claude builds, tests, documents, **stops before commit**.
4. Owner uploads Claude report to ChatGPT; ChatGPT reviews financial integrity, sync/deletion, backward compatibility, test/browser evidence, risks. Request tightly scoped fixes if necessary.
5. After explicit approval, Claude reruns `npm test`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, checks working-tree scope, commits/pushes `main`, confirms successful deployment, reports SHA/status.
6. Conduct remaining real-device/offline/backup acceptance. **Refresh this handoff and `docs/BACKLOG.md` as part of every completed phase/release and material architecture decision**, never after the fact only.
7. Do not start the next phase until it is authorized.

**Source-of-truth priority for changing facts:** live source/code and current Git + Actions > most recent completed test/build evidence > latest dated phase report > current backlog > this handoff's last snapshot > old conversation context. For **decisions**, latest expressly approved owner decision takes priority, with deviations documented. If sources disagree, say so and reconcile deliberately; do not silently overwrite history.

## 5. Architecture invariants

### Local-first and data ownership

- Browser app with IndexedDB/Dexie; version 15 at H3. Cross-module module records (Tasks/Projects/Assets) use the shared `moduleRecords` system; shared `links` point to stable identifiers. No database table or schema change has been needed for Assets H1–H3.
- Backup, restore and encrypted sync work over existing tables; new module-record kinds/fields ride established mechanisms. Sync is generally per-record last-writer-wins, with **narrow deletion precedence** rules for Projects and Assets. The app's plain export is **not encrypted** and can contain sensitive asset data; warn accordingly.
- No passwords, API keys, household exports, bank balances, actual serials/VINs/addresses, or private document links in GitHub docs/tests/prompts; repo is PUBLIC. Use entirely invented fixtures.
- Use existing Contacts records for people/providers, not duplicate vendor lists; existing Task recurrence and Calendar layers for due items; Finance owns transactions/valuations; Projects owns commitments/financial summaries; Assets owns physical assets/service history/warranties.

### Financial integrity

- Money stored in **whole cents**; Finance transaction balances, projects attribution and net worth use existing engines (`src/lib`); read them, don't recreate them in Assets.
- Project spending attribution supports whole and split transactions, budget lines, commitments, change orders and signed values. P4A established multi-line commitments and reconciliation; P4B/P5 integrated reporting and visualizations.
- H3 asset purchase/service expense is **one source at a time**: typed amount *or* refs to one/more existing Finance transaction pieces, never silently both. Switching source requires explicit confirmation; blank cost means unknown, not zero.
- H3 ref shape: `txnId`, optional `splitIndex`, explanatory snapshot `{date,payee,amountCents}`, and for split refs fingerprint of split count, amount, category. Finance splits **lack stable IDs**; changed/missing/removed/moved split refs require review and contribute nothing until reselected; do not use snapshot as live spend. Identical split parts with matching amount/category cannot be distinguished; documented limitation. Whole-transaction refs use current live Finance amount and flag changes.
- **B5 (approved by the B5 brief, to be confirmed at review):** a household-wide figure never recalculates money: it calls the existing readers (`householdSpend()`, `billsRecordedPayments()`, all on `spend()`) unchanged and merges their resolved pieces by `pieceKey`. One payment is one payment however many records link it; the breakdown credits it to one module or "Shared between modules", never by list order; module totals are shown non-additively with a reconciliation. A typed amount is never deduplicated against a Finance payment or another typed amount by value, date, payee or contact; a possible duplicate is a hint (same amount, dates within 7 days, the same provider or a `bills-for` link) that never changes a total. Commitments, expected payments and recorded spending are three separate figures and are never summed. A figure is never called reconciled while a stale link, a possible duplicate, a disputed typed amount or a typed payment with no amount is unresolved. A linked bill and arrangement fold into one commitment only on exact equality of complete amounts. The overview is read-only and never a Calendar authority.
- Spend totals computed on read; refunds reduce spend, transfers excluded, transaction pieces deduplicated across records and hierarchy. Whole transaction supersedes overlapping constituent split references within the same aggregate. Manually typed amounts cannot be deduplicated by coincidence.
- Project paid-so-far is shown **separately** on assets as context; never add Project paid totals to direct asset spend (avoid double counting). Existing net-worth `properties` stay Finance-owned; Assets may link read-only to one.

### Sync, deletion, history

- Projects permanent deletion: block if financially referenced; cleanup links without erasing Tasks/Events; narrow merge policy resolves stale edits while safeguarding financial dependencies.
- Assets H1: archive vs stored/sold/disposed/replaced status vs permanent deletion are different. Asset deletion uses tombstone/`permanentlyDeletedAt`; deletion wins stale edits unless live dependent child/history/reference needs preserving. `revivedAt` makes intentional backup restoration possible. A completed linked Task and live service/warranty records prevent deletion; H4 verified warranty deletion guards with real sync-engine tests. Replacement links are validated against cycles. Older app builds may lack the special merge rule; real-device testing remains important.
- H2 maintenance plans are **ordinary linked Tasks**. A durable Asset `service` record holds date, kind, work, provider, optional cost, notes, document refs, origin task ID and title snapshot; it remains after task edits/reopens/deletes. "Done, log it" joins Tasks' own `completeTask` with service write in one Dexie transaction; repeat occurrence created once and retains asset link. Task completed elsewhere appears "not yet saved to history" until explicitly logged.
- **H2 final follow-up:** duplicate records created for the same originating task on two offline devices are **preserved** and flagged for human review; no automatic deletion/merge; Undo can restore a record and warning. After-completion repeats use the entered actual service date as basis, while fixed repeats use the normal completion-day basis (no rewriting general Task recurrence).
- **Deleting a Finance transaction (ST-S2, invariant):** it goes through `planTxnDeletion` / `applyTxnDeletion` (`src/lib/txnDeletion.ts`). The owner sees which records in other modules point at it; nothing is deleted, edited or repaired in those modules; no typed amount is substituted; the apply plans again inside one database transaction and refuses (deleting nothing) if anything differs from the preview or any reader cannot run. Finance never imports a household module: each module contributes a read-only `contributes.txnReferences` reader (`docs/module-hooks.md`). A deletion on one device and a new link on another leaves a VISIBLE stale reference after sync, never a silent repair. A new module that stores a Finance reference must add a reader.
- Real physical two-device/offline/older-version behavior has **not** been fully acceptance-tested at H3; simulations using the real sync engine are reported. Never conflate simulations with field verification.

### Privacy/UX

- Assets may store optional address, serial/VIN and plate; mask until deliberately shown and do not expose in list titles, Search snippets, Calendar/ICS or Today. Public docs use hypothetical data only. External document references are safe HTTP(S) metadata, not uploaded file storage.
- Rich UI must be accessible and responsive. Tests and 375px browser checks are required; label any unverified visual/keyboard/screen-reader paths rather than claiming success.
- **Accessibility invariants (ST-S3, keep):** every screen renders ONE `main` through `src/shell/Main.tsx` (id `main-content`; the skip link and the route announcer depend on it; a test forbids a raw `<main>`); a page has one `h1` (pages nested in Settings use the `PageHeadingLevel` context for an `h2`); card titles are `h2`. A control in a row or table is named for its PURPOSE and RECORD, and **no spoken name or `title` carries a money figure** where Hide Amounts applies (`src/lib/a11yLabels.ts`). Errors are words, announced (`role="alert"`, or `status` for a background failure), and tied to their field with `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby`. Words that sit on a colour's own light tint use the `--accent-text`, `--warn-text`, `--pos-text`, `--neg-text` tokens (`src/ui/tokens.css`), never the bare colour (a test fails otherwise). Tab and radio groups use `src/ui/rovingKeys.ts`. `ConfirmDialog` only asks: the CALLER must still run its own safety check inside `onConfirm` (the account dependency re-check, the stale-preview re-plan), and clearing Sync conflict history must go through `removeConflicts` with the keys that were shown. The device-settings explanation (`src/lib/devicePrefs.ts`) is checked against `PREFERENCE_KEYS`; a new stored preference must be added to it.

### Delivery and offline (ST-S4)

- A module's **registration** (its `ModuleDef` contributions and address list) is always in the first download; only its **screens** load on demand through `lazyModule`. Nothing in a screens file may register anything at import time. `src/modules/lazyBoundary.test.ts` walks the static imports from `main.tsx` and fails if a lazy module's screens, a Ledger page or Settings becomes reachable. New modules follow `docs/module-hooks.md` ("Loading a module's screens on demand").
- **Offline must keep working.** The build writes the exact list of built files and a fingerprint into `dist/sw.js`; installing saves every one. A worker without its placeholders fails the build. One cache per build, the build before kept, older removed; only caches whose name starts `ledger-` and that are not another copy's are touched (the address is shared with other apps; never delete caches by a broad pattern). Do not hard-code hashed filenames anywhere.
- Do not use React `lazy`/`Suspense` for module screens: React holds a revealed loading state for about 0.3 s, which made opening a saved module 20 times slower (measured). A tab two releases behind a deploy may show "did not load" for a screen it never opened (Reload Ledger fixes it); this is accepted and documented.

- **Version skew and sync (ST-S5 finding ST5-F1, FIXED in ST-S5.1, COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED `ba4da59`, run #79; a device is protected only once it has installed that build):** the merge is told the tables of its own build (`known`); any other table is carried through untouched and its ids are never remembered, so an older device cannot erase a newer build's rows. **Still do NOT add a database table (a Dexie version bump with a new store) until ST-S5.1 is deployed AND installed on every device**: a device still on `4b827eb` or older still records unseen rows as deleted. (Family Management F0 plans NO new table, so this does not block it.) New modules use the shared `moduleRecords` table (no migration). Unknown FIELDS on known records are safe. A sync file whose format `v` is not 1 is refused before anything changes, so a future format change must bump `v`. The conflict list (ST5-F2, FIXED in ST-S5.1) is changed through `MetaStore.update`, one IndexedDB transaction, which is safe across tabs; `MetaStore.update` is required (no fallback) and must stay so. Historical wording: the ST-S5 finding was "serialized per tab, not per browser".

Household Services, Bills & Subscriptions and the Family member and dates pages recomposed on the shared Signature components (date tiles, status chips, figures, two-column layouts with a hero on each detail page). Presentation only: every figure, date, cadence, label, relationship and safeguard equals the pre-change value (the structure read from the app's own functions is identical before and after, and browsing wrote no row). Services: date-grouped upcoming visits, arrangement cards, hero and two-column arrangement, provider and visit pages, a read-only "Coming up after that" list from `plannedVisitDates`. Bills: Coming up beside Expected cash needed (Known and Estimated separate), key-terms band, date-led list, one week-grouped Upcoming card. Family: hero member page with the next-birthday tile and Related tasks, month-grouped dates. Evidence: 49 scripted browser checks, 380-cell sweep, keyboard walk, empty states, baseline and after values identical. Not built: the needs-review page recomposition, anything in the hard scope limits. Report: `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D15-REPORT.md`.

### Ledger Signature D13.1, Today design-fidelity refinement, 2026-11-12 (BUILT AND TESTED LOCALLY; UNCOMMITTED, NOT STAGED, NOT PUSHED, NOT DEPLOYED; awaiting review; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

Today rebuilt to the approved D3.1 composition (presentation only): Next up bar, status band (Overdue, Today, Coming up), a wide agenda column (Today, Reminders due, Overdue with module strips and red date tiles, Coming up with a date gutter and one-line rows) and a narrow side column (Needs attention, Cash outlook, a "Household status" panel holding Bills, Household finances, Home & Assets, Household services and Projects). Columns appear when the page area is 880 px or wider (container query); a phone gets a compact header, three-line rows and chevron action buttons. Customization is a small Today-specific model (`composition.ts`, key `today.layout.v2`): fixed agenda order, reorderable side sections, any card hideable, no widths, Reset, and the earlier `today.layout.v1` carried over in memory with a dismissible note and never written. Next up keeps its D11 rule; the label adds "after today" when today still has items. Verified: identical rows and counts before and after, 25 desktop and 20 phone browser checks, keyboard walk, themes, empty profile. **Final mobile correction:** on a phone (640 px and narrower) Coming up shows its first three populated days whole with a "Show all N items" button (real count; "Show fewer" when expanded; ordinary button, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, focus to the first revealed row, not saved), and Cash outlook is drawn once after Overdue (rule in `composition.ts`; saved layouts never rewritten); desktop pictures are byte-identical to before; phone page 3,382 px collapsed (was 4,319; D11 5,040; prototype 2,668); full suite 4,962 tests in 265 files. **Open decisions:** chronological Next up (options B and C), dropped widths, a Bills due or cash cell in the band, Family names on rows.

### Ledger Signature D10, Quick Add redesign, 2026-11-12 (BUILT AND TESTED LOCALLY; UNCOMMITTED, NOT STAGED, NOT PUSHED, NOT DEPLOYED; awaiting review; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

The Add box now has the approved D3.4 layout and a phone bottom sheet; behavior is unchanged. **Structure:** `AddBox.tsx` is the controller (state, `routeQuickAdd`, preview, the one `commit`, open and close); `AddBoxView.tsx` only draws what it is given and imports no module code; `addBoxModel.ts` holds the pure arrangement (the three featured destinations, groups derived from `groupModules` and each destination's `moduleId`, primary-action and feedback rules, `resolveDestination`, the two specialised actions); `sheetBehavior.ts` holds `sheetInsets`, a counted `lockScroll` and `attachSheet` (visual-viewport listeners and the scroll lock, only while a `Modal variant="sheet"` is mounted; the phone query is `(max-width: 640px), (pointer: coarse)`). **Invariants kept:** no Contact is created, found or linked by Quick Add (guard tests); Transaction keeps its own form and Save (no footer Save); the open-a-screen destinations keep `openCopy` wording and close-then-open order; a record is saved in exactly one place. **Review points:** Bill or subscription under Household; compact sheet about 66 percent of the screen (content-driven); the token clear button; the page lock also applies on large touch screens. **Not verified:** real phones, screen readers, forced-colors, other presets, Safari and Firefox. Report `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D10-REPORT.md`.

### Ledger Signature D9, Family member color picker and identity system, 2026-11-11 (COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED `939f985`, Pages run #95 success, verified at the D10 gate; the "pending" wording below is superseded; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

See the top notice and `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D9-REPORT.md`. **Approved decisions D9 followed:** the D3.4 Family design (eight named swatches, preview, No color row, ring in the main text color, two columns on a computer); the eight stored hex values stay authoritative and nothing is migrated, added or auto-assigned; no uniqueness rule, conflict warning or "used by" badge (one polite sentence only); Calendar colors stay independent; Contact identity safeguards unchanged; American English for new user-facing text. **Contract for later phases:** (1) a person's color is still stored only as `color` on the Family member record (one of the eight hex values or absent); (2) to draw it, convert the stored value with `memberColorId` (`src/modules/family/memberIdentity.ts`) and pass the id to `MemberMark`, `MemberDot` or `MemberChip` (`src/ui/MemberIdentity.tsx`); the shared pieces take a semantic id only (no hex, no module imports, because a Family guard forbids other modules importing Family; a later phase that needs Family data in Calendar or Tasks needs a decision first, not a workaround); (3) the colors come from the `--mc-*` tokens (lighter in dark themes; `--swatch-check` is the check mark ink) and `[data-mc="<id>"]` sets `--pc`; (4) the pieces are decoration (`aria-hidden`): always print the name or the color name beside them; (5) "no color" draws a neutral gray, never a stored color; (6) member colors are NOT shown in Calendar, Tasks or Today and are never a Calendar layer color (`FAMILY_LAYER_COLOR` stays `#c4398a`); (7) the form is read by tests as markup, and the picker is native radio buttons: do not replace them with a custom widget; (8) Gold and Teal fills are under 3:1 on pale older palettes, so swatches and dots carry a darker outline (do not remove it); (9) the Family overview (D12) and member page (D15) are still to be redesigned and may use these pieces. **Unknown:** real-device and screen-reader behavior; one test more than D8's count plus 63 (not investigated). **AI roadmap:** unchanged (deterministic presentation only; no new data flow, no AI interface).

See the top notice and `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D8-REPORT.md`. **Contract for later phases:** Ledger's own navigation is `LedgerNav` (900 px and up) drawn from `ledgerNavSections()` in `src/shell/ledgerNavModel.ts`, which reads `GROUPS` in `src/shell/nav.ts`; a new Ledger page is added to `nav.ts` (and the route lists) only, and the bar, phone tabs, breadcrumbs and search follow. The bar is sticky at `top: var(--bar-h)` with height `--sub-h`; the page top bar and the table headings sit at `--bar-h + --sub-h` (+ their own offsets), so any new sticky Ledger layer must add `--sub-h`. `--lnav-h` and `--ui-lnav-ic` are Signature-only tokens (defaults 40 px, no icons). Colours are `--side-bg`, `--side-fg` and `--side-active`, so those three palette roles now colour this bar (the palette builder still labels and previews them as a sidebar, to be fixed in D19). The phone's section tabs and bottom tab bar are in `Shell.tsx`, unchanged. `sideState.ts` (the old open-group memory, key `ledger-side-open`) is no longer used by the app. Do not make the section links open lists on hover, and do not turn the lists into an ARIA menu without a new decision: the contract is link + separate disclosure button.

### Contacts U2, Contact creation identity safety, 2026-11-10 (BUILT AND TESTED LOCALLY; UNCOMMITTED, NOT STAGED, NOT PUSHED, NOT DEPLOYED; awaiting independent review; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

- Report `docs/CONTACT-IDENTITY-U2-REPORT-2026-11-10.md`. **Contract:** `resolveContactChoice(d, { contactId } | { name, phone?, email?, relationship?, confirmedDifferent? })` in `src/core/contactIdentity.ts`. An id must be a live Contact (never replaced by a same-name one). A new name that matches a live Contact (`nameKey`, never fuzzy) throws `SameNameContactError` with every match and writes nothing, unless `confirmedDifferent`, which creates a SEPARATE Contact through `createDistinctContact`. It joins a caller's transaction. Bills' and Services' `ProviderInput` is `ContactChoice`; Projects has `addNewVendor` and `CommitmentFields.newVendor` (the Contact is made in the same transaction as the commitment). `src/ui/ContactChoice.tsx` holds `ContactSelect`, `NewContactBox`/`NewContactBoxView`, `ContactField` (deferred), `NewContactNow` (Home & Assets). `logStandaloneVisit` gained `providerNameOnly`. **Quick Add never creates, finds or links a Contact** (provider name kept as `providerNameSnapshot`; previews say "saved as a name only, no contact linked"); the Bills suggestion form no longer pre-selects a Contact from a payee name. **Invariants kept:** no schema, migration, sync or backup change; C2 readers, guarded removal and recovery untouched and passing; no merge, no id rewrite, no edit of an existing Contact. `findOrCreateContact` is deprecated and unused by application code (a test pins it); `src/core/contactTestkit.ts` (`providerNamed`) is test-only.

### Family Management F4, 2026-11-09 (COMMITTED AND PUSHED after final release verification; the "uncommitted" wording in the paragraph below was written before release and is superseded; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

- Report `docs/FAMILY-MANAGEMENT-F4-REPORT-2026-11-09.md`. **Contract:** read-only aggregation over existing records for the ACTIVE, readable household members (one person per Contact); `readHouseholdOverview(today, visibility)` reads Family members and dates, Contact NAMES only, Calendar events and layers, and Tasks, each once and row by row with the owning module's parser; pure builders `buildHouseholdActivities`, `buildHouseholdTasks`, `buildHouseholdDates`, `forPerson`. **Activities:** `event.contactIds` contains a member's Contact id; window today through today + 6; Calendar `occurrencesOf` (skipped and moved dates follow the Calendar); identity `<eventId>:<original date>`; one row listing every member; calendars switched off on this device (the Calendar's `calendar-hidden-layers` preference via its exported `readVisibility`) are counted, never listed; a stored Birthday event on the same day as the same Contact's Family birthday is not repeated as an activity. **Responsibilities:** `task.contactIds` contains a member's Contact id; Tasks' `isOpen`, `isOverdue`, `sortTasks`; overdue first, then dated, then undated; wording "Associated with" (Tasks has no assignment field); no completion or editing. **Dates:** F2's `upcomingRows` unchanged (leap day 28 Feb, active members only), today through today + 30. **Filter:** drop-down, Everyone default, page state only (not stored or synced), narrows all three sections identically. **Today:** no card (Today already reads the Family due provider). **Not built:** Search, Quick Add, Today card, the F0 "Needs attention" counts, overview "Important contacts" and "Initiatives" blocks, the `audience` parameter (deferred to the Command Station design), F5.

### Contacts & Relationships C2, 2026-11-09 (BUILT AND TESTED LOCALLY; UNCOMMITTED, NOT STAGED, NOT PUSHED, NOT DEPLOYED; awaiting independent review; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

- Report `docs/CONTACTS-RELATIONSHIPS-C2-REPORT-2026-11-09.md`. **Contract:** still the one shared `contacts` table and stable Contact ids; no new table, field, migration, sync or backup format, dependency or backend. **References (layer 2):** `contributes.contactReferences` (`src/core/contactReferences.ts`, documented in `docs/module-hooks.md`): each module reads its own stored Contact fields and returns named rows (title, relation in words, date, status, an address in the owning module's own deep-link format, a facet). Readers: Family (member, important contact), Calendar, Tasks, Projects (project, commitment), Home & Assets (asset, service, warranty), Household Services (arrangement provider and extras, visit), Bills (provider). Read only (hook-verified), no Finance table, damaged rows skipped one by one, never by name or provider-name snapshot. The Contact page shows them grouped with a preview of five and "Show all" (hard cap 100); when the complete numeric scan (layer 1) finds more records than can be named, or a reader cannot run, the page says so ("other stored references may exist") and never reads that as unused. **Directory:** a Show filter (All, Household members, Important Family contacts, Service providers, Project participants, No confirmed references) from stored relationships only; "no confirmed references" needs both layers clear and every reader to have run; it never authorizes removal. **Picker:** the Calendar and Task "People" box now chooses by Contact id, labels two same-name Contacts apart (details and "2 of 3 with this name"), and creates a new Contact only through `addPickedContact` (`createDistinctContact`): a taken name creates nothing and shows the matches, "Use this one" per match, and a tick before a separate Contact is made. **Removal:** `src/core/contactRemoval.ts`: a Contact is removable only when the layer-1 scan, the older text rule and every named reader all find nothing and every reader ran; plan (preview), then apply in ONE transaction over contacts, moduleRecords, links, attachments and dueItems that re-checks and compares a fingerprint, then a soft delete (same row, never hard-deleted) with a guarded Undo (same id). If another device adds a reference before the removal reaches it, both records are kept and the directory lists the "removed but still in use" Contact with a Restore button; Ledger does not claim race-free deletion. **Family:** `family/references.ts` and the member page are unchanged (deliberate separation, compat test). **Still name-matching (NOT changed, pinned by a test):** Assets (`AssetForm`, `ServiceForm`, `WarrantyForm`), Bills (`data.ts`, `discoveryData.ts`), Projects (`data.ts`, `CommitmentForm`), Services (`data.ts`).
- **Known gaps:** not real-device or screen-reader tested; the Task editor dialog is about 38 px wider than a 320 px screen with or without the picker (found while testing, pre-existing, not changed); the directory computes every module's named reader on each data change (about 0.9 s for 1,500 Contacts and 15,000 records in Node; browser timing unmeasured); a Contact made in the picker exists as soon as it is added, even if the event or task form is then cancelled (as before; it can be removed from Contacts); retired (handed-over) Calendar events are listed without a link.
- **Not built:** C3, F4, Command Station, merge, tags, types, notes, interaction history, Quick Add for Contacts, fixing the other name-matching creation paths.

### Contacts & Relationships C1, 2026-11-09 (COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED `eafae83`, Pages run #84 success; the "uncommitted" wording in this heading was written before release and is superseded; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

- Report `docs/CONTACTS-RELATIONSHIPS-C1-REPORT-2026-11-09.md`. **Contract:** the shared `contacts` table is the only Contact identity; the Contacts module writes it only through `createDistinctContact` (never name matching) and `updateContactDetails`; it has no records, fields, deletion, archive, merge or notes. **Edit safety:** no-op saves write nothing; a save whose `expected` version (updatedAt plus the four details) differs from the stored Contact is refused inside the same transaction; unknown fields and the unused `notes` are preserved. **Usage:** `contactUsage.ts` counts records that mention a Contact id (numeric match; may over-count; never authorizes deletion, merge or relink). **Family:** `spouse` role; the `isMe` mark with the two-mark conflict rule. **Invariants held:** Dexie v15; no Finance import or table; screens on demand; Contacts imports no other module and no module imports it except the module list; nothing shared or displayed.
- **Known gaps:** the Calendar and Task `ContactPicker` still merges by lower-case name (C2); no named reference lists (C2); no deletion, archive or notes (C2/C3); an older device shows Spouse as "needs review".
- **Not built:** N1, C2, C3, F4, Command Station.

### Family Management F3, 2026-11-09 (COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED `0469af6`, Pages run #83 success; the "uncommitted" wording in this heading's original text was written before release and is superseded; NOT REAL-DEVICE TESTED)

- Report `docs/FAMILY-MANAGEMENT-F3-REPORT-2026-11-09.md`. **Contract:** a person is tied to a Calendar event or Task only through the `contactIds` those records already have; Family reads them (read-only readers that skip an unreadable row instead of failing) and adds no field to Calendar, Tasks or Projects. Schedule window is fixed at 30 days and uses the Calendar's expander and its device-local switched-off-calendars preference; To do uses Tasks' open/overdue/sort rules; Projects uses `vendorContactIds` only. The one new record kind is `family/contact` (fields exactly `memberId`, `contactId`, `purpose`, `status`; no Contact detail copied; archive not delete; duplicate key = member + Contact + purpose ignoring case/accents; unreadable rows counted and kept; unknown fields carried).

### Projects P1–P5 — finished and deployed

- P1/P2/P3: project registration, planning and tasks/milestones, budgets/commitments and management workflows; consult detailed reports for exact features.
- **P4A:** agreement allocation across budget lines, change orders, payments/transaction tagging and reconciliation; pushed `1c478e0`.
- **P4B:** safe project permanent deletion, `+project` Quick Add, bulk task project assignment, task/milestone summaries in project list, project labels in Search, project-filtered Finance trends; sync deletion precedence with safeguards for monetary dependencies; pushed `6545763`.

### Home & Assets H0 — architecture

- One generic asset kind (`property`, `area`, `system`, `appliance`, `vehicle`, `other`) with optional parent pointer, specs, contacts, warranty dates, refs and lifecycle. No forced hierarchy; parent link and cycle/depth checks.
- Planned phases H1 registry; H2 maintenance/service records; H3 Finance/Projects linkage; H4 warranties/retirement/replacement/orphan repair; H5 overview/Today/final acceptance. H0 Revision 2 superseded an earlier suggestion to omit deletion-wins policy for Assets.
- Owner confirmed eight recommended “A” defaults (reference rather than mutate Finance, durable maintenance records, restrictive deletion, separate net-worth entries, masked sensitive details, minimal Projects-side integration, editable generic maintenance suggestions, route/name Home & Assets).

### H1 — asset registry (deployed `bf2d3d5`)

- `#/assets`: flat/tree lists; in-use/stored/past/archived filters; asset add/edit with optional make/model/identifiers, acquisition/warranty info, shared Contacts, specs, external document refs; parent hierarchy and validation; links from Tasks/Events; Search and Quick Add prefix `a`; masking; archive/retire/restore and safe permanent deletion; narrow Assets merge rule. H0.5 prerequisites: improved sync conflict labels and plain-backup warning.
- At H1 review: 1,407 tests passing.

### H2 — maintenance and durable history (deployed `f101e1b`)

- Asset maintenance from linked recurring Tasks with editable generic suggestions; atomic "Done, log it"; unscheduled service logs; service-history timeline; "Save to history" for tasks completed elsewhere; provider Contacts, typed costs, refs; service Search and Quick Add `svc`; deletion guards and sync tests. Follow-up: duplicate-warning and historically correct recurring-task basis.
- At H2 final review: 1,464 tests passing.

### H3 — Finance references and Project links (deployed `30118dd`)

- Read-only Finance transaction/split selection for asset acquisition and service records; typed-or-Finance source switching with explicit confirmation; split fingerprint/review; spend summary and parent rollups with piece deduplication; Related Projects and Related Assets cards; Project -> asset prefilled service-record handoff requiring explicit save; read-only net-worth entry pointer. Minimal Projects UI file additions; Finance, Projects financial calculations and sync implementation unchanged.
- At H3 review: 1,521 tests passing, 97 files; `tsc` and build green. Browser interacted with flows using isolated data but **no screenshot visual review**; pending visual/light-theme/a11y, stale ref replacement, handoff Save, unlink/Undo, refund UI, real phone/two devices/offline.
- GitHub verified H3 pushed/deployed on 2026-11-06, even though H3 report and top backlog status were written before commit.

### H4 — warranties and lifecycle (deployed `89ffde9`)

Report: `docs/HOME-ASSETS-H4-REPORT-2026-11-06.md` (final follow-up 2026-11-06). Built atop deployed H3 `30118dd`. **At H4 final review: 1,586 passing tests across 100 files**, TypeScript and production build clean; Dexie 15; no Finance/Projects/Tasks/Contacts source modifications or backup/sync format changes. Committed and pushed as `89ffde9`; Pages run #57 succeeded.

- Multiple durable warranty records with shared Contacts and safe document links; H1 simple warranty fields act as **implicit fallback** only when no live explicit warranty exists. Deleting last explicit warranty prompts whether to clear legacy fields; no silent deletion.
- Fifth read-only Calendar layer `9_000_000_000_000_105` derives warranty expiration markers. Stored and active assets show markers; archived/sold/disposed/replaced do not; expired markers are informational, not overdue. Maintenance reminders stay Tasks-owned.
- Atomic asset replacement with existing/new asset and review step; new asset gets only name/type/place, never copied sensitive identifiers or history. Existing service/financial/warranty/Projects history remains with old asset.
- Previewed retirement/bulk retirement of property descendants; open tasks explicitly Keep linked (default), Move, Stop repeating or Delete. A shared Task's **Delete** choice now warns it will be deleted for all linked assets; this was the final follow-up fix, covered by a focused test.
- Past-asset navigation, replacement links, orphan service/warranty/link detection and explicit re-home/clear/remove handling; protects absent-on-this-device sync cases.
- Real sync-engine tests cover warranty versus deletion, replacement conflicts, orphan repair and backup revival. **Actual two-device/real-phone/offline and complete keyboard/screen-reader testing remain outstanding**.
- Known limitation: simultaneous independent replacement on two devices may leave both created replacement assets, only one referenced as successor; other can be archived. Last writer wins per record. Other outstanding browser checks include some warranty editing/document rows, Undo, Stop repeating/Delete actions and shared-delete warning display.
- **Handoff procedure:** installed 2026-11-06 (`docs/CHATGPT-HANDOFF.md`, rule appended to root `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/CLAUDE-HANDOFF-SOP.md` kept as the source text) and committed with H4.

### H5 — overview, Today and acceptance (COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED `4422999`, Pages run #58; real-device acceptance NOT DONE)

Report: `docs/HOME-ASSETS-H5-REPORT-2026-11-06.md` (including its final follow-up, the Modal keyboard tests). Built on deployed H4. **1,629 passing tests across 102 files**, `tsc` and build clean; Dexie 15; no Finance, Projects, Tasks, Contacts or Calendar source edits; no schema, backup or sync change.

- `#/assets` now opens an **Overview** (Needs attention, Upcoming maintenance, Warranties ending soon, Recent service history, Spending on assets); the list is "All assets" at `#/assets/list`. One read-only reader (`overviewData.ts` → pure `overview.ts`) feeds both the page and the Today card; nothing is stored. It reuses Tasks' `isOverdue`, H4's `warrantyStatus` (60 days) and `effectiveWarranties`, H2's `unsavedTasks` and `duplicateTaskGroups`, H4's `detectOrphans`, and H3's `spend()` unchanged.
- Spending: household total counts each payment once; a year narrows by each piece's own date (Finance payment by transaction date, typed cost by service/purchase date, undated typed costs only under All time); per-property rows can exceed the household total when one payment is linked in two properties (stated on screen); unknown cost is never $0; stale references are left out and counted; project money is never included.
- Today card `assets.attention`: counts only, half width, hides when empty, no names/amounts/identifiers, does not list maintenance tasks (Tasks already do).
- Archived and sold/disposed/replaced assets raise no attention (same rule as Calendar warranty markers); their history and spending still count.
